# Ancestry dossiers — consolidated source material

This document combines the two uploaded dossiers. Repeated claims are retained where they add detail; discrepancies are flagged in the worklist rather than silently reconciled.

## A. Comprehensive ancestry research dossier

COMPREHENSIVE ANCESTRY RESEARCH DOSSIER & MASTER LEDGER
Source Tree: Slægten Gårdsted og Gedde (https://sloev.github.io/tree/)
Target: Complete individual profiles with dates, locations, occupations, 
children, works, and image/document URLs for agent retrieval.


---
## Section I — INTRODUCTION & FAMILY BRANCHES OVERVIEW
---
This dossier compiles the genealogical records of the user's family tree, 
encompassing four primary ancestral lines as documented in church books, 
censuses, local archives, and family papers:
1. Farfar's Side (Djursland, Mols, and Copenhagen): 18th-20th century agrarian and urban families.
2. Farmor Liss' Side (Vestjylland / Heath parishes - Assing, Sønder Felding, Sønder Omme): 18th-19th century pioneers and farming families.
3. Morfar's Side (Nybøl, Egernsund, Lejrskov, Officersslægten Gedde, and Pomeranian noble house von Kleist / von Woyten): Military officers, nobility, and Southern Jutland lineages.
4. Mormor Gerda's Side (South Langeland and the Langø family): Coastal and agricultural lineages of Langeland.

---

## B. Expanded master dossier: legitimate, illegitimate, foster and alliance lines

EXPANDED MASTER ANCESTRY DOSSIER: LEGITIMATE & ILLEGITIMATE BLOOD, MARRIAGE, 
ADOPTION, AND HISTORICAL CONNECTIONS
Source Tree: Slægten Gårdsted og Gedde (https://sloev.github.io/tree/)
Document Ref: expanded_master_ancestry_dossier.txt


---
I. SCOPE & METHODOLOGICAL NOTE ON NON-TRADITIONAL & ILLICIT LINEAGES
---
Genealogical research in Danish parish registers (Kirkebøger), census records 
(Folketællinger), probate records (Skifteprotokoller), and military/adels archives 
frequently reveals complex family dynamics that extend beyond formal nuclear 
marriages. This dossier explicitly incorporates:
1. Legitimate Bloodlines: Standard marital births and generational descents.
2. Illegitimate & Extramarital Bloodlines ("Uægte Børn"): Births recorded out of 
   wedlock where biological paternity is established via church baptismal records, 
   paternity cases (Almendings- og Sognefogedprotokoller), or court documents.
3. Acknowledged Paternity & Social Fatherhood: Instances of step-parenting, 
   fosterage (Plejebørn), and recognized illegitimate children who carry biological 
   bloodlines.
4. Adoptive & Feudal Alliance Ties: Legal and social adoptions, land transfers, 
   and dynastic marriages across Danish, Pomeranian, and German nobility.

---

# Archived file: leads/potential-leads-register.md

# Potential genealogical leads — research register

Research date: 16 November 2026

## How to use this register

This file deliberately preserves potential, unverified, contradictory and partially sourced leads. A lead is not a fact merely because it appears here. Preserve the exact wording and provenance, assign a status, and revisit it when new evidence becomes available. Do not delete weak leads simply because they are unresolved; annotate negative searches and explain what was searched.

Suggested statuses:
- **Documented clue** — a source explicitly names the person, event or relationship, but the original underlying record may not yet have been inspected.
- **Strong lead** — a credible source or index points to a specific record or relationship.
- **Hypothesis** — plausible but not yet directly supported.
- **Conflict** — sources disagree or identity/relationship is ambiguous.
- **Open search** — a defined archival or online search remains to be done.

## A. von Kleist — Muttrin and Damen branches

### A1. Dubislaf / Dubislaff “Bratta” on Damen

**Status:** Documented clue from the 2025 family-association genealogy; underlying medieval enfeoffment should still be inspected.

The 2025 *Geschichte des Geschlechts v. Kleist*, Muttrin-Damensche Linie, Damen volume, says:
- Dubislaf, also called Bratta, on Damen, is dated 1477 and died circa 1482.
- He was the son of Henning (I.M.8) and Margarethe von Wedelstädt of Gülzow.
- A collective feudal letter dated 13 April 1477 mentions him and five sons: Drews, Curt, Bisprow, Peter and Pribislaf.
- It says Dubislaf held Damen and half of Bublitz; his brother Bisprow held the other half.
- It describes two marriages: first to Catharina von Podewils, mother of Drews, Curt and Bisprow; second to an unnamed von Glasenapp of Gramenz, mother of Peter and Pribislaf.

Source: https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/Damen.pdf, especially printed pages 2–3 / PDF text around lines 89–136.

**Important:** This is a family genealogy compiled from older family history and documentary references. It is a strong roadmap, not a substitute for checking the cited 1477 feudal letter (reference 125), 1486 enfeoffment (reference 192), and the source notes.

### A2. Prissebur of Muttrin and the 1364 document

**Status:** Documented clue; the family association's 2025 Muttrin volume explicitly cites a 1364 document.

The 2025 *Muttrin* volume says the second son of Klest (M.1) appears in document 83 of 1364 as “Prißebur, der Klest heißt von Muttrin”. It says the names Jarßlaff and Henning as Prissebur's sons come from a family genealogy of 1576 and an older letter concerning Glineke. It then identifies a Henning of Muttrin in the 1407 material.

Source: https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/Muttrin.pdf, introduction, PDF text around lines 223–260.

The older L. Quandt genealogy (published in the family history) discusses the early descent and its reasoning, including the difficulty of dating the alleged earlier family letter and the 1364 Prissebur reference:
https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Genealogie/genealogie.pdf.

### A3. Keep the two claims distinct

The 2025 *Damen* volume says Jarislav brought the Damen estate into the family around 1365. That is not the same claim as Prissebur appearing in a 1364 document as a Muttrin landholder. The family history treats Muttrin and Damen as branches/estates within the wider Muttrin-Damensche line.

Sources:
- Damen volume: https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/Damen.pdf
- Muttrin volume: https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/Muttrin.pdf

Do not state that Prissebur personally acquired Damen in 1364. Do not conflate the Muttrin line's early generations with the later Damen branch without tracing the numbered family-tree references.

### A4. Remaining medieval uncertainty

The older Quandt reconstruction itself contains argued identifications and inferred generations. The 2025 volumes explain that the early generations rely on contemporary documents together with later family records and a “probable arrangement” of persons. The medieval pedigree should therefore be represented with source-specific confidence, not as uniformly proven descent.

Potential next checks:
1. Obtain document 83 (1364) and inspect the original name, property and witness context.
2. Obtain document 125 (13 April 1477) and document 192 (28 December 1486) cited in the Damen volume.
3. Compare the 1576 family genealogy with the later 19th-century family history and the 2025 revision.
4. Keep any “Prisse Kleistes genannt” woman in the 1577 settlement separate from Prissebur of Muttrin unless a source proves a connection.

## B. Gaardsted / Rolsøgård

### B1. Karen Christensdatter and Claus Gaardsted

**Status:** Strong lead for a sibling relationship; parentage of Claus and Karen remains unresolved.

The Rolsøgård G302 alphabetical probate index, under Niels Jensen Degn in Vrinners, probate no. 11, 14 May 1787, folio 39, includes this note:

“Arv i boet til afdødes stedbørn efter Christen Christensen Tohde? foged i Dejret (skifte 1764) til datter Karen Christensdatter LV: broder Claus Gaardsted forpagter på Vosnæsgård.”

This supports that Karen Christensdatter was identified as sister of Claus Gaardsted, tenant farmer at Vosnæsgård. It points to an earlier 1764 probate for Christen Christensen Tohde, bailiff in Dejret.

Source: https://slaegt.dk/media/jnnnvuml/alfabetisk-navneliste-og-skifteuddrag-til-rols%C3%B8g%C3%A5rd-gods-g302-mols-herred-1778-1816-1818.pdf.

**Caution:** The index wording says “afdødes stedbørn efter Christen Christensen Tohde” (the deceased's stepchildren after Christen Christensen Tohde). It does not establish that Tohde was Karen's biological father; it may indicate step-parentage. The 1787 probate is Niels Jensen Degn's probate, not Karen's own probate. The index is a pointer to a 1764 case, not the original case itself.

### B2. The 1764 Tohde probate

**Status:** Open archival search.

Locate the earlier Rolsøgård estate probate series for 1764 (before the indexed 1778–1816 volume). Search under Christen Christensen Tohde, possible spelling variants, and Dejret. Establish:
- identity and marital history of Tohde;
- names and relationships of his stepchildren;
- identity of their biological parent(s);
- whether Claus Gaardsted is explicitly named as Karen's brother and how his own parentage is described.

Do not assign “Christensen” as Claus's proven patronymic or Tohde as his father until the original 1764 probate or another primary record supports it.

## C. Gedde / Giedde

### C1. Hans Christopher Gedde

**Status:** Strong published-genealogy lead; primary records still desirable.

Carl Johan Anker's 1885 biographical entry identifies Hans Christopher Gedde as born 24 June 1738, died 9 March 1817, son of Danish generalmajor Samuel Christopher Gedde (born 1691, died 1766) and a wife née Picheneau de la Ramil-liére. It names two marriages: Frederikke Christiane Fischer, then on 15 February 1789 Ødegaard Sophie Fischer. The entry identifies sons Wilhelm N. Gedde and Ove Samuel Gedde.

Source: https://arkivkopia.se/bok/runeberg-acj330bio.

**Caution:** Anker gives Ove Samuel's birth as 28 May 1788, conflicting with the 28 February 1778 date in Harbou's 1897 article and the current tree. Do not use Anker alone to change Ove's date.

### C2. Samuel Christoffer and Christian Giedde

**Status:** Published genealogy lead; birth year of Christian not yet confirmed.

The Anker entry gives Samuel Christopher Gedde as born 11 July 1691 and died 1766, and names his father as a ritmester Christian Giedde, who died in 1705. The claimed 1638 birth for Christian has not been verified in the source inspected here.

Source: https://arkivkopia.se/bok/runeberg-acj330bio.

Further source to examine: *Norske Slægter* (1912), which may give a separate genealogy of the Gedde family. Treat it as a compiled secondary source until its references are followed.

### C3. Ove Samuel's birth and baptism

**Status:** Conflict over birth date; primary baptism record open.

Harbou's 1897 article in *Personalhistorisk Tidsskrift* gives Ove Samuel Gedde as born 28 February 1778 and died 10 March 1843:
https://v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf.

Anker's 1885 biography gives 28 May 1788:
https://arkivkopia.se/bok/runeberg-acj330bio.

The current tree's 1778 date agrees with Harbou, but the Fredensborg Slotssogn baptism register should be inspected to resolve date and parentage. Search the 1778 baptism entries and check adjacent entries and any alternate Hof- und Slotsmenighed register references. Record the actual wording, not just an indexed transcription.

### C4. Hans Christopher's two marriages

**Status:** Published clue; exact spellings and dates require original marriage records.

Anker records Frederikke Christiane Fischer as first wife and Ødegaard Sophie Fischer as second wife, with the second marriage dated 15 February 1789. Verify the exact name spelling (Ødegaard/Øllegaard) and the sisters' birth/death details against parish and burial records before normalizing the names.

## D. Later Danish / Swedish / German research leads

### D1. Lovisa Larsson, Malmö, 1862

**Status:** Open search; user-supplied lead.

Reported as a daughter of Niels Larsen, born in Malmö in 1862. Search Malmö parish birth records and household examination/moving records for Lovisa/Lovisa variants and Nils/Niels Larsen. Confirm exact parish, birth date, mother's name and subsequent identity before connecting to the existing tree. This lead has not yet been independently checked.

### D2. Wilhelm Krogh, 9 October 1908, “Tastrup/Vastrup,” Kreis Flensburg

**Status:** Open search; place-name conflict.

Reported birth date 9 October 1908 and a place transcribed as “Tastrup/Vastrup” in Kreis Flensburg. The place name is uncertain and should be preserved as two candidate readings rather than silently standardized. Search relevant German civil registration and church registers only after locating the historical locality/Standesamt jurisdiction. This may be relevant to the Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen paternity investigation, but no identity or parentage link is established by the lead alone.

## E. Emigration and overseas descendants

### E1. Jørgen Christensen and Bodil Margrethe — nine children in America

**Status:** Open search; user-supplied family-tree lead.

The GEDCOM reportedly names nine children and gives America as destination. Preserve all nine names and their original birth details from the GEDCOM; search Danish emigration protocols, US passenger arrivals, census records, naturalization, draft registrations, marriage and death records. Do not assume all nine travelled together or that “America” means the United States rather than another destination.

### E2. Iver Peder Holgersen — Fresno, 1910

**Status:** Open search; user-supplied lead.

Reported destination Fresno in 1910. Search the Danish Emigration Archive/Copenhagen police emigration protocols for the original contract, then California passenger, city directory, census, naturalization and death records. Verify whether 1910 is departure, arrival or first known residence.

### E3. Emil Gedde — Argentina, 1922

**Status:** Open search; user-supplied lead.

Search emigration/passenger records and Argentine civil, immigration and local records. Preserve spelling variants (Gedde/Giedde) and verify whether 1922 is departure or arrival.

### E4. Olaf Gedde — Shanghai, 1904

**Status:** Open search; user-supplied lead.

Search Danish emigration and passport records, shipping/passenger lists, consular and commercial directories, local newspapers and records in Shanghai. Verify whether the stated destination is a final destination or an intermediate port.

**Research method:** Rigsarkivet notes that Copenhagen police emigration protocols from after 1868 only include people who purchased a contract ticket through an emigration agent; independent travellers may be absent. The Danish Emigration Archive provides searchable records for the 1868–1920 period. Sources:
- https://en.rigsarkivet.dk/guide/new-to-genealogy-get-started/
- https://slaegt.dk/kom-i-gang/saerlige-problemstillinger/ind-og-udvandring/
- https://slaegt.dk/media/1cwl3fn5/19052025-den-store-udvandring-pdf.pdf

## F. Sophus Petersen's reported fourth daughter

**Status:** Open search; partial parish-book work reported by user.

Three daughters reportedly found in Nybøl church books for 1938, 1940 and 1943; a fourth daughter is said to have gone to Norway but has not been located. Recheck the full family group and relevant Nybøl birth/baptism registers across a wider date range. Search emigration, Norwegian census, parish and civil records once the daughter's full name and approximate birth date are established. Do not infer that a missing fourth daughter existed solely from the count unless the source for that count is recorded.

## G. Vibygård probate

### G1. Hans Jensen, gardener at Vibygård

**Status:** Open archival search; user-supplied lead.

A probate dated around 1820 is reportedly associated with Hans Jensen, gardener at Vibygård. Identify the relevant estate jurisdiction and probate series, then inspect the original probate for spouse, children, residence, employer and any family relationships. Search spelling variants and estate records for Vibygård. No relationship to the current tree is established yet.

## H. Rosmus identity conflict

### H1. Mette Pedersdatter (Hallis) versus Mette Sørensdatter Helles

**Status:** Conflict; unresolved in prior notes.

Two different candidate identities appear in the Rosmus research. Preserve both candidates and the exact source statements that support each. Compare original baptism, confirmation, marriage, census, probate and children's baptism records. Do not merge the two women or select one merely because a compiled tree gives one version.

## I. Evidence and repository policy

- Keep user-supplied assertions as leads, labelled as such, with the originating file or message and any source citation available.
- Preserve negative searches, source conflicts, spelling variants, uncertain places and alternate identities.
- Separate biological, legal/acknowledged, social/step, adoptive, foster/guardian and household relationships.
- A source index or compiled genealogy can establish that a clue exists; it does not automatically prove every relationship inferred from it.
- Add people to the canonical data only after the relationship and identity are adequately supported and the source citation is attached.
- Do not delete a lead merely because it remains unproven.

# Archived file: leads/investigation-worklist.md

# Investigation worklist extracted from uploaded ancestry files

**Status:** leads extracted; no genealogical assertions changed in canonical data. Priorities below reflect the strength of the stated source anchors and the importance of resolving identity/parentage conflicts.

## Priority 1 — Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen: competing biological-father claims

- **Person:** Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen (born 12 January 1932, Egernsund; died 6 May 2009, Svendborg).
- **Official record claim:** Egernsund civil birth register, 1932 no. 4, reportedly records a marginal note dated 11 July 1932 recording Wilhelm Krogh of Snogbæk as the legally acknowledged/registered father, based on a declaration dated 5 April 1932 and a court decision dated 10 June 1932.
- **Family-history claim:** biological father was Sophus Petersen (born 1903, Påkjær, Nybøl; died 24 August 1945); Wilhelm Krogh allegedly took responsibility; Peter Frederiksen was the social/stepfather and gave Lorenz his surname.
- **Existing source anchors:** Egernsund civil birth register (BSID 37658, image 125); Broager Vestre baptism register 1933 no. 1 (BSID 201141, image 112); family document “Paula Mathilde Chrestle Gedde og Jens Jensens slægt”; family chart; grave photo.
- **Research tasks:** obtain and read the complete civil birth entry and marginal note; locate the 5 April 1932 declaration and 10 June 1932 court judgment/case file; search paternity/civil court records and any appeal/correction; inspect the original baptism; independently establish Sophus Petersen's whereabouts and household in 1931–32 and test the alleged four daughters/half-sibling clue. Keep legal paternity, biological paternity, and social fatherhood as separate fields.
- **Do not:** replace the official record's named father with Sophus based only on family tradition.

## Priority 2 — Kresten Henriksen Jørgensen, illegitimate birth (1877)

- **Person:** reported born 3 November 1877; described as an illegitimate son of Lars Thomsen Larsen (born 1 November 1859) and raised by the mother's parents.
- **Claim source:** family chart; partly obscured handwritten entry; no mother or original parish citation supplied in the export.
- **Research tasks:** identify the mother's name and parish from the original chart; search 1877 birth/baptism registers in Magleby and neighbouring South Langeland parishes; inspect illegitimate-birth columns, acknowledgements, alimentary support cases and confirmation records; trace Kresten in 1880/1890/1901 censuses and confirm whether “Henriksen Jørgensen” is a recorded patronymic or later name.
- **Do not:** assume fatherhood from the obscured chart alone.

## Priority 3 — Ove Samuel Gedde: birth-date and maternal-identity conflict

- The existing project note records 28 February 1778 and parents Hans Christopher Gedde and Øllegaard Sophie Fischer.
- The uploaded dossier only gives a broad 18th-century description.
- Prior research in `research_method.md` notes an 1885 secondary biography (Carl Johan Anker, p. 94; unproofread OCR) apparently giving 28 May 1788 and stating that Hans Christopher's first wife Frederikke Christiane Fischer died in 1787 and second marriage to Ødegaard Sophie Fischer occurred 15 December 1789.
- **Research tasks:** establish the correct parish from military/service and family records; locate original baptism; inspect Hans Christopher's marriages, wife's death and household chronology; compare the 1778 and 1788 candidates without silently normalizing Øllegaard/Ødegaard spelling.
- **Do not:** alter birth date or mother without original evidence.

## Priority 4 — Dybbøl p53 / Anne Christine Hansen marriage (1866)

- Existing worklist concerns the reported 18 September 1866 marriage of Rasmus Jensen and Anne Christine Hansen and collateral testing of Anne's proposed parents Hans Hansen and Ellen Kock.
- Exact target image remains unlocated; Arkivalieronline viewer previously returned only a shell. The Dybbøl Ab 4 (1861–1927) inventory says the volume contains marriages 1861–1906.
- **Research tasks:** obtain visual access to the marriage index and correct register page; transcribe witnesses, residences, ages and fathers; independently test Hans Hansen and Ellen Kock in collateral records.
- **Do not:** infer Anne's parents from secondary online trees or reuse the unrelated 1864 page 169 reference.

## Priority 5 — Gedde / von Kleist / von Woyten lineage and medieval chain

- Uploaded profiles name Christina von Woyten (c.1600), Georg von Kleist (c.1595), Hedwig Maria von Kleist, Colonel Christian von Kleist (c.1620), Cartz Ulrik von Kleist (c.1670), Cartz Ulrik von Gedde (III.369; Danish service from 1701; died Rendsborg 1722), and Pribislaff von Kleist (d.1698).
- The dossier cites H.W. Harbou (1897), the Muttrin-Damensche history, the von Kleist document book, Zedler's Universal-Lexicon, and C.E. Schøller's Gedde officer genealogy.
- Explicit caveat in the export: the chain from Pribislaff (d.1698) through the Borntin branch to medieval Prissebur / Klest de Densin is not yet proven. Medieval references (1248, 1289, 1364, 1477) are historical context, not established direct descent.
- **Research tasks:** inspect cited works page-by-page; build a generation-by-generation table with exact quotations/page references; compare Harbou with newer register; inspect 1477 fief letter and earlier documents; verify each parent-child link independently.
- **Do not:** add medieval persons as direct ancestors until the chain is demonstrated.

## Priority 6 — Rousing ancestry (18th–19th century)

- Known outline: Rasmus Jensen Rousing (sognefoged) → Anders Rasmussen Rousing (1818–1886) → Rasmine Christine Andersen (1855–?) → Hans Peter Gaardsted.
- Earlier links are marked uncertain: Jens Rasmussen (?) and Rasmus Jensen (c.1718, ?); maternal line of Anders not recorded.
- **Research tasks:** locate Rousing household and parish records around Fuglslev; verify the bailiff office; identify the father of Rasmus Jensen Rousing and mother's line; resolve the question-marked generations through parish/census/probate records.

## Priority 7 — Vestjylland / Pårup Melgaard

- Jens Peder Larsen, born 29 February 1812 in Assing, died 25 May 1870 in Sønder Omme; married Kjersten Frederiksdatter on 8 October 1843 in Sønder Felding. The uploaded dossier's Mette Pedersdatter spouse claim conflicts with the tree and Horsbøl index; see research notes.
- Dossier mentions foster children (plejebørn) and collateral illegitimate connections in heath parishes, but supplies no names or exact entries.
- **Research tasks:** inspect 1843 marriage; identify all children in parish registers and census; compare 1870 Sønder Omme census with Horsbøl collection; record foster children as social relationships, not biological children.

## Priority 8 — Djursland / Kolind / Rosmus collateral branches

- Dossier proposes Niels Jensen (“Degnen”, 1857–1932) and Karoline Dam Jensen (1863–1947), parents of William Valbjørn (1896–1965), grandparents of Sigrid Valbjørn (born 15 July 1926); it mentions out-of-wedlock baptisms in Kolind and Rosmus collateral branches.
- **Research tasks:** identify exact parish entries and occupations; map parentage of William; locate the alleged collateral illegitimate baptisms and document them individually. The supplied material is too broad to treat as a verified family line.

## Priority 9 — South Langeland / Langø

- Marius Michelsen Christensen (1858–?) and Ane Marie reportedly bought Langø in 1894; causeway construction began 11 January 1911 and reached 334 m. Their daughter Kristine Adamine Margrethe Christensen (1884–1975) married Axel Erik Holgersen (1882–1962); daughter Karen Margrethe Holgersen (1906–1992) married Laurits Edvard Larsen (1898–1980); their daughter Gerda Frederiksen (1935–2019).
- **Research tasks:** verify land purchase through deeds/auction/estate files; locate 1911 municipal/road authority permissions and contemporary newspaper reports; corroborate memoir “En Langø-piges erindringer” against land records and census.
- **Do not:** assume all colourful memoir details are independently corroborated.

## Priority 10 — Magleby / Christian Hansen and Marie Madsdatter

- tree2 notes Christian Hansen (born c.1806) married Marie Madsdatter (born c.1807) on 13 November 1829 in Magleby; no parents or exact register image are supplied.
- **Research tasks:** find marriage entry, transcribe witnesses/residences/ages; follow each spouse into baptism, census and probate sources; check whether the pair are part of the Langø line or a collateral family only.

## Additional data-integrity issues to preserve

- Two distinct people share identifier `p13`: Else Gedde Jensen (Egernsund line) and Lovisa Larsson (Jørgensen line). Resolve before any ID-based merge.
- The Bornholm Lars Larsen (born 1771 Rutsker, died 1854 Allinge) is explicitly rejected by the source as an erroneous automatic link; do not reintroduce.
- The export claims 486 total people, 156 ancestors, 14 generations, earliest birth 1654, 329 siblings/descendants/in-laws, 593 source references and 157 people shown publicly. Treat as snapshot metadata, not current database counts.
- Living people are redacted; no decryption or private data access is authorized by these files.
- Some names/dates are derivative, broad or uncertain. Every lead needs its own source-level verification.


## Corrections from first-source audit (16 November 2026)

- **Jens Peder Larsen's spouse:** the uploaded Expanded Master Dossier says Mette Pedersdatter. The current tree's ancestry narrative and Horsbøl's genealogy instead identify Kjersten Frederiksdatter, born 20 April 1822, married 8 October 1843 in Sønder Felding. Horsbøl lists their daughter Mette Marie; this may explain the dossier's confusion. Verify the original marriage entry before changing any record.
- **Ove Samuel Gedde:** P.I. Liebe and E. Borgstrøm's officer biography (based on Rigsarkiv military notes) gives 28 May 1778–10 March 1843. Carl Johan Anker's 1885 work gives 28 May 1788, but Project Runeberg marks that OCR page as unproofread. The project data says 28 February 1778. The year 1778 has stronger support; exact day/month remains unresolved pending the original baptism.
- **Lorenz paternity:** the repository's own narrative says the Egernsund civil register names Wilhelm Krogh as father through a declaration and court decision, while the family hypothesis names Sophus Petersen. The tree currently encodes Sophus as biological father but explicitly admits this is family knowledge and that DNA is needed to decide. Do not call the case legally or biologically settled until the actual paternity file and evidence are reviewed.
- **Langø:** Langeland Byarkiv's catalogue confirms the 2011 article “Langø og dæmningen” by Lisbeth Skov: Marius Christensen and Ane Marie bought Langø in 1894; the 334 m causeway was built in 1911; Ane died in 1930 and Marius in 1934. The same archive catalogue identifies real-register and land-book material for Lindelse and Magleby (1790–2013), a promising route to verify title.
- **Kresten Henriksen Jørgensen:** an exact-name web search and a check of the Strømming public K-name index found no match. This is only a negative index result, not evidence that the person or baptism does not exist. Original Magleby and neighbouring parish registers remain the required next step.

# Archived file: leads/research-notes.md

# First investigation notes — uploaded ancestry leads

**Date:** 16 November 2026  
**Repository:** `sloev/tree`, `main` only  
**Scope:** initial audit of the four uploaded files and targeted source checks. This is a research checkpoint, not a completed proof of every line.

## 1. Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen — legal and biological paternity must be separated

The current canonical narrative (`family-tree.md`) gives two incompatible accounts:

- The Egernsund civil birth register (1932 no. 4) reportedly has a marginal note dated 11 July 1932 recording land worker Wilhelm Krogh of Snogbæk as the legally acknowledged/registered father, based on a paternity declaration of 5 April 1932 and a court decision of 10 June 1932.
- Family knowledge instead identifies farmer's son Sophus Petersen of Påkjær, Nybøl, as Lorenz's biological father and says Wilhelm accepted paternity. The narrative explicitly says the tree follows family knowledge and that only DNA can decide the biological question.

The current data model has Lorenz (p6) linked to Sophus as father and Peter Frederiksen as stepfather. That is a recorded project hypothesis, not a conclusion demonstrated by the civil-register marginal note.

### Archival route now identified

Rigsarkivet's current guide for 1919–1970 says a paternity case began with the police; if paternity was disputed, the civil court decided it, while the maintenance/alimentation case is held in the county archive. It recommends starting with the county archive and searching:

1. `Alimentationsjournal` (and any register to it);
2. `Almindelig Journal`;
3. `Journalsager` / `Alimentationssager`.

If the case is not located there, search the civil court's case list and judgment book, then police paternity protocols. The guide states that paternity/alimentation case material is subject to a 75-year access period.

The official Landsarkivet for Sønderjylland archive overview says the Aabenraa and Sønderborg counties were merged into **Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt effective 1 April 1932**. Its archive description includes alimentations cases and identifies registers/journals/journal files for 1920–1935, with the relevant unprinted finding aid held in the reading room. Since the reported judgment is 10 June 1932, Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt is the first county archive to investigate, not the pre-merger Sønderborg Amt archive.

**Important limitation:** the exact case, journal number, judgment, and underlying evidence have not yet been located. This is a precise archive route, not a claim that the files have been read.

Sources:
- Rigsarkivet, “Faderskabssager – Kom godt i gang!”: https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/faderskab-og-adoption/faderskabssager-kom-godt-i-gang/
- Rigsarkivet, *Landsarkivet for Sønderjylland: En arkivoversigt* (2002), pp. 91–92 / PDF lines 6228–6325: https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/Landsarkivet-for-Soenderjylland-En-arkivoversigt.pdf
- Project's current source narrative: https://github.com/sloev/tree/blob/main/family-tree.md

## 2. Jens Peder Larsen — spouse correction

The uploaded Expanded Master Dossier identifies Mette Pedersdatter as Jens Peder Larsen's spouse. This conflicts with both the current project narrative and the Horsbøl genealogy index.

Horsbøl identifies:
- Jens Peder Larsen, born 29 February 1812 at Pårup Melgaard, Assing; died 25 May 1870 at Sønder Omme, Kirkeby; buried 2 June 1870.
- Kjersten Frederiksdatter, born 20 April 1822 at Tarp, Sønder Felding.
- Marriage: 8 October 1843 in Sønder Felding.
- A child named Mette Marie is listed beneath the couple.
- Kjersten later appears in a second marriage dated 11 June 1872 to Niels Pedersen.

The uploaded “Mette Pedersdatter” spouse name may be a confusion with daughter Mette Marie. This is a secondary-source correction only; the original 1843 marriage register should still be transcribed before canonical data is edited.

Sources:
- Horsbøl, genealogical entry: https://www.horsboel.dk/familie/grind/dat16.htm (lines 415–439 in the opened page)
- Existing project narrative: https://github.com/sloev/tree/blob/main/family-tree.md

## 3. Ove Samuel Gedde — birth date discrepancy narrowed

Three dates are present across the source material:
- Canonical project data: 28 February 1778.
- P.I. Liebe and E. Borgstrøm, *Biografiske oplysninger om faste artilleriofficerer af linien...*, printed p. 509: 28 May 1778–10 March 1843. The title page says the work was compiled from Captain Georg Lutken's notes in the Rigsarkiv's Army Archive.
- Carl Johan Anker, *Biografiske data om 330 norske...* (1885), p. 94: 28 May 1788–10 March 1843. Project Runeberg explicitly labels the OCR page “never proofread”; the scan should be visually checked before treating the printed 1788 as certain.

The year 1778 is supported by the officer biography and the project's broader family narrative. The exact day/month remains unresolved: 28 February (project) versus 28 May (officer biography). The 1788 year in Anker conflicts with the 1778 officer biography and the project's date, and should be treated as a likely secondary-source discrepancy pending inspection of the original scan and baptism.

Sources:
- P.I. Liebe & E. Borgstrøm, *Biografiske oplysninger om faste artilleriofficerer af linien*, printed p. 509: https://slaegtsbibliotek.dk/910571.pdf
- Carl Johan Anker (1885), p. 94, unproofread OCR: https://runeberg.org/acj330bio/0110.html
- Existing canonical data: https://github.com/sloev/tree/blob/main/data/build_data.py

## 4. Langø — local-history claim corroborated; title record still needed

Langeland Byarkiv's register of articles in the yearbook *Øboer* lists “Langø og dæmningen” by Lisbeth Skov (2011) and summarises the claims:
- Marius Christensen and his wife Ane Marie bought Langø in 1894.
- Marius, with help from sons and a son-in-law, built a 334 m causeway in 1911.
- Ane Marie died in 1930; Marius remained on Langø until his death in 1934.
- Their children erected a memorial stone in 1934.

This is useful published local-history corroboration, but the article's family connection means the land transaction should still be checked against the title record. Arkiv.dk describes Rudkøbing Byhistoriske Arkiv collection A790 as containing real registers and land books (1790–2013) for Lindelse, Magleby and other Langeland parishes.

Next: request the Lindelse real-register entry and associated 1894 deed/tingbog entry; then search local authority or newspaper records for the 1911 causeway.

Sources:
- Langeland Byarkiv, “Register over artikler i Øboer”: https://byarkivet.langelandkommune.dk/boeger-brochurer/register-over-artikler-i-oeboer
- Arkiv.dk, collection A790: https://arkiv.dk/vis/3931656

## 5. Kresten Henriksen Jørgensen — initial null result

The upload states that Kresten Henriksen Jørgensen was born 3 November 1877 as an illegitimate son of Lars Thomsen Larsen (born 1859) and was raised by the mother's parents. The note says the family-chart entry is partly obscured by an ink blot; no mother or original register reference is supplied.

An exact-name web search and a check of the Strømming public index under K did not identify this person. The K index lists four people named Kresten, none matching the supplied name. This is not a comprehensive search of original registers and does not disprove the claim.

Next: inspect the original family chart; identify the mother and likely parish; search Magleby and neighbouring South Langeland birth/baptism registers for 1877, including illegitimate-birth columns; then follow confirmation and census entries.

Source checked:
- Strømming public K-name index: https://www.stroemming.dk/genealogy/gedweb/Public/da/Lists/allnames-K.html

## 6. What remains unverified

- No original paternity file or DNA evidence was obtained.
- No original 1843 marriage register image was transcribed for Jens Peder and Kjersten.
- No original baptism was located for Ove Samuel to resolve 28 February versus 28 May.
- No deed or land-book image was inspected for the 1894 Langø purchase.
- No original 1877 baptism or family-chart image was inspected for Kresten.
- The medieval von Kleist chain remains explicitly provisional as stated in the source dossier.

No canonical person records or relationships were changed in this pass. No private/encrypted files were accessed. No build or tests were run.

# Archived file: leads/tree-research.md

# Genealogical research notes from `tree.txt`

**Status:** reviewed and normalised; selected facts added to the tree source data  
**Scope:** Christina von Woyten, the Pomeranian von Kleist line, and additional branch-expansion leads  
**Rule:** only claims supported by a traceable source are entered as family relationships. Hypotheses and unsourced suggestions remain research leads.

## 1. Verified additions made to the family tree

The English translation of the von Kleist family history, *History of the Family von Kleist – Tychow-Dubberow line*, page 123, states that:

- Georg von Kleist of Groß-Tychow (died after 1645) was married to Christina von Woyten.
- Christina was the widow of Otto von Below of Pustamin before her marriage to Georg.
- Christina was the daughter of Benedictus von Woyten of Versin, princely Pomeranian captain of Bütow, and Elisabeth von Stojentin of Giesebitz.
- Georg and Christina had one daughter, Hedwig Maria, who married Colonel Christian von Kleist (II. 60).
- Georg's feudal estates passed to his son-in-law Christian.

The existing tree already contained Georg, Christina and their daughter Hedvig/Hedwig Maria. The source data has now been extended with:
- Benedictus (Benedict) von Woyten, linked as Christina's father.
- Elisabeth von Stojentin, linked as Christina's mother.
- Otto von Below of Pustamin, recorded as Christina's first husband.
- A specific source entry for the von Kleist family archive translation.

**Primary source:** von Kleist Family Archive, [History of the Family von Kleist – Tychow-Dubberow line (English translation), p. 123](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf).

## 2. Existing tree context

The source data already records:
- Georg von Kleist (II. 55), of Groß-Tychow, died after 1645.
- Christina von Woyten as Georg's wife.
- Hedwig Maria von Kleist as their daughter.
- Colonel Christian von Kleist (II. 60) as Hedwig Maria's husband.

The family archive passage supports those relationships. It also states that Georg left no legitimate heirs other than Hedwig Maria.

## 3. Research leads — not yet added as proven ancestry

### 3.1 Earlier von Woyten ancestry

The supplied dossier proposes this paternal sequence:

1. Michael von Woyten — described as an ensign in the service of Emperor Charles V.
2. Conrad von Woyten.
3. Conrad Pantaleon von Woyten.
4. Vandemar von Woyten.
5. Carl Woiten — said to have flourished around 1400.

It also names Martin von Woyten as a great-uncle of Benedictus.

These names and links require direct checking against the cited Zedler article and other contemporary records. The dossier does not provide page/column references or transcriptions sufficient to enter the parent-child links. They have therefore **not** been added to the tree.

### 3.2 von Stojentin maternal ancestry

The dossier suggests that Hans (Johann) von Stojentin (1446–1517), lord of Gohren and Landvogt of Stolp, may have been Elisabeth von Stojentin's father. It further names Peter von Stojentin (c. 1400–1470) and Elisabeth von Lettow as Hans's parents.

The dossier itself describes the connection to Elisabeth of Giesebitz as probable rather than demonstrated. No relationship has been added. The estate association with Giesebitz is not, by itself, proof of parentage.

### 3.3 Medieval von Kleist ancestry

The dossier proposes an early line beginning with:
- Klest de Densin, mentioned in 1289.
- Prissebur, reportedly mentioned in 1364.
- Jarßlaff, whose sons are reportedly mentioned in 1407.
- Henning, reportedly alive after 1435.
- Bisprow, reportedly enfeoffed in 1477 and dead around 1493.

It then refers to later Muttrin-Damen-line figures, including Pribislaff and Hans Jacob von Kleist (born 1654). The material mixes family numbering systems and does not establish a continuous, evidenced chain from the medieval names to the later individuals already in the tree. None of these people or links has been added.

**Research target:** inspect the original Muttrin-Damen family-history documents and the document references/footnotes cited by the family archive. Treat each generation separately; a family name or estate succession is not enough to establish biological descent.

### 3.4 Gedde / Gjedde

The dossier identifies:
- Ove Gjedde (1594–1660), son of Brostrup Gjedde and Dorthe Ulfeldt.
- Brostrup Gjedde (c. 1560–1614).

The existing tree already has an established Gedde officer lineage, but the supplied notes do not provide precise primary-record citations for these proposed links to the current branches. Do not attach them to the existing tree solely on the basis of the dossier. Verify against C. E. A. Schøller's *Stamtavle over Officersfamilien Gedde (Gjedde)* and contemporary records first.

### 3.5 Other branch-expansion suggestions

The dossier mentions possible research avenues for:
- Djursland / Valbjørn / Jensen / Gårdsted families.
- West Jutland families around Assing, Sønder Felding, Sønder Omme and Pårup Melgaard.
- The Langø family around Magleby and Nordenbro on Langeland.

Names and dates in these sections are not accompanied by individual record images, archive identifiers or sufficiently precise citations. They remain search leads, not verified additions.

## 4. Claims to resolve before further additions

- The supplied notes give inconsistent lists of Christina's sisters and use both Anna and Catharina. Do not add a sibling group until the underlying family record is checked.
- The proposed Hans von Stojentin → Elisabeth von Stojentin parent link is explicitly uncertain.
- The medieval von Kleist sequence needs source-by-source reconstruction, including checking whether similarly named Pribislaffs belong to different branches.
- Dates expressed as “mentioned in”, “flourished”, “alive after” or “died before” should be represented as dated events or date ranges, not converted into exact birth/death dates.

## 5. Source bibliography and next checks

1. **von Kleist Family Archive**, *History of the Family von Kleist – Tychow-Dubberow line*, English translation, p. 123. [PDF](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf). Used for the verified Christina–Georg–Hedwig relationships and Christina's parents/first husband.
2. **H. W. Harbou**, “Slægten von Kleist i Danmark,” *Personalhistorisk Tidsskrift*, 3rd Series, vol. 6 (1897), pp. 95 ff. [PDF](https://www.v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf). Already cited in the tree for the Danish von Kleist lineage.
3. **Johann Heinrich Zedler**, *Grosses vollständiges Universal-Lexicon*, vol. 59 (1749), article reportedly headed “Woyden, Woyten, Woiten, Woyda, Woyd.” [Digitised work catalogue](https://ered.library.upenn.edu/cgi-bin/res/fullview.cgi?resID=10178). The exact article/page and the claims attributed to it still need to be checked.
4. **C. E. A. Schøller**, *Stamtavle over Officersfamilien Gedde (Gjedde)*. Locate and inspect the relevant edition and page references before using the proposed Gedde links.
5. **Danish National Archives**, relevant parish registers, censuses and probate records. Use exact archive/image identifiers in the tree rather than unspecific references to a parish or database.

## 6. Editorial policy

- Keep this file as a research log; it is not an import file.
- Add a person or relationship to `data/build_data.py` only when a source supports that specific fact.
- Preserve uncertainty in the data model. Do not turn hypotheses into biological parent links.
- Record the exact page, document number, archive reference or image identifier for each new claim.
- When a new source contradicts an existing claim, document the conflict before changing the tree.


## 7. Review of the November 2026 ancestry uploads

Two further files were added in commit [d3be503](https://github.com/sloev/tree/commit/d3be503f544aef3f015b724367cc898b4358016c):

- [`complete_ancestry_dossier.txt`](https://github.com/sloev/tree/blob/d3be503f544aef3f015b724367cc898b4358016c/complete_ancestry_dossier.txt) — narrative overview of the Gedde/von Kleist/von Woyten, West Jutland, Djursland/Mols/Copenhagen and South Langeland branches.
- [`tree2.txt`](https://github.com/sloev/tree/blob/d3be503f544aef3f015b724367cc898b4358016c/tree2.txt) — a text export describing the existing site data, generations, people, source references, images and documents.

### 7.1 What the uploads add to the research workflow

The files are useful as an index and reconciliation checklist, not as independent proof:

- The dossier provides a compact branch-level map and identifies people/topics for further research, including Jens Peder Larsen and the Pårup Melgaard / Assing–Sønder Felding–Sønder Omme area.
- The export supplies a convenient inventory of the existing tree's people and source pointers, including Danish parish-register, census, local-history and family-document references.
- The export lists nine image files and points to the GEDCOM, JSON, family-document data and printable posters. These are repository/site assets, not additional independent historical sources.
- The modern Danish branches are already represented in the structured export. Re-entering people from this text would risk duplicate individuals and duplicate or conflicting relationships.

### 7.2 Reconciliation cautions retained from the export

The export itself flags several important data-quality issues. Preserve these when using it:

- The identifier `p13` is used for two different people in the export: Else Gedde Jensen and Lovisa Larsson. Do not use that identifier alone to merge records or infer a relationship.
- The Bornholm Lars Larsen (born 1771 in Rutsker, died 1854 in Allinge) connection is explicitly rejected by the source notes as an erroneous automatic link. It must not be reintroduced.
- The medieval von Kleist material is contextual and the continuous descent is not established. Do not convert the names in the narrative into direct ancestors without a documented generation-by-generation chain.
- Some people have partial dates or incomplete event details. Preserve unknown values as unknown; do not fill gaps from approximate narrative descriptions.
- The export reports aggregate counts (including 486 people and 593 source references), but those counts are descriptive metadata, not a validation of every person, event or citation.
- Living-person information is intentionally withheld from the public export. Do not attempt to reconstruct it from encrypted material or expose private identities.

### 7.3 New claims deliberately not imported

No new people or parent-child links have been added to `data/build_data.py` on the strength of these two files alone.

In particular:
- The dossier's expanded claims about Christina von Woyten's children (“and others”) conflict with the family-archive passage already cited above, which identifies Hedwig Maria as Georg and Christina's only legitimate child. Retain the archive-supported statement unless a stronger primary source demonstrates otherwise.
- The profile dates and places for several early modern noble figures are approximate and lack pinpoint citations in the uploaded dossier.
- Broad descriptions of the Djursland, West Jutland and Langeland branches do not identify individual records precisely enough to create or modify relationships.
- The dossier's references to military rolls, local-history collections and census material are leads only until the exact record, page/image and relevant entry are inspected.

### 7.4 Recommended next verification sequence

1. Reconcile each person in the new narrative against the existing structured data by name, dates, spouse/parent context and source identifiers; never match on `p13` alone.
2. For the modern Danish lines, use the exact church-book, census, probate or local-archive reference already attached to the relevant person. Verify the actual record image and transcribe the event before changing the data.
3. Treat the nine image files as illustrations or document leads. Where an image is a source document, record its archive origin, date, page/image number and the fact it supports.
4. For the Pomeranian lines, continue from the cited family-history works and contemporary feudal, church or military records, documenting each generation separately.
5. Only after verification, update the source-of-truth script and regenerate the structured JSON. Keep this research log separate from importable genealogical data.

**Editorial conclusion:** these uploads improve navigation and expose useful source leads, but they do not independently justify additional structured ancestry links. The previously verified Christina von Woyten relationships remain the only new Pomeranian parentage/partnership additions supported by the cited family-archive passage.


## 8. Genealogical scope: all well-founded family connections

**Research scope is not limited to legitimate descent or conventional pedigree lines.** The objective is to document real, well-founded connections among people and families, including connections that historical records may have marginalised, obscured or described inconsistently.

Include, where supported:
- Biological parentage and descent, whether the child was born within or outside marriage.
- Acknowledged, recorded or otherwise well-supported illegitimate children and non-marital paternity/maternity.
- Marriage, remarriage, partnerships and other documented unions, including relationships that overlap in time.
- Adoption and legally or socially recognised parent-child relationships.
- Step-parent, foster, guardian and household relationships where they are historically meaningful.
- Other documented connections (for example, a recognised fatherhood, maintenance, probate, inheritance or guardianship relationship) that help explain the family network.

### Relationship labels and evidence

Never collapse different kinds of connection into a single generic parent/spouse link. Distinguish at least:
- **Biological** — asserted or supported biological parentage.
- **Marital/partner** — marriage or documented partnership; does not itself prove biological parentage.
- **Adoptive** — adoption or legally recognised adoptive parentage.
- **Step** — relationship through a parent's marriage/partnership, without implying biological descent.
- **Foster/guardian/household** — care or household connection, not biological descent unless separately evidenced.
- **Acknowledged/social/legal parent** — a person recognised as a parent in a source, with biological status separately stated if unknown or disputed.

For every proposed relationship, record what the source actually establishes. A baptism entry naming a father, a probate statement, a court case, a family chronicle, a marriage record and a later family tree do not all prove the same thing. Note whether a source establishes biological parentage, legal recognition, social parenthood, marriage, or merely a claim.

Use calibrated confidence and preserve disagreements:
- **Established**: direct, credible evidence for the specific relationship.
- **Probable**: strong converging evidence, but not conclusive.
- **Possible/claimed**: a plausible lead or a claim that needs corroboration.
- **Disputed/rejected**: conflicting evidence or a link shown to be erroneous.

Do not treat illegitimacy as a reason to exclude a person or connection. Do not infer illegitimacy from silence, stigma, a gap in records, or a later surname. Equally, do not turn a historical allegation or rumour about fatherhood into a fact without evidence. Use sensitive and respectful wording, quote historical terminology only when necessary to explain a source, and distinguish a contemporary allegation from a proven biological relationship.

### Data-model implications

Before adding complex or overlapping relationships, inspect the data model and rendering. The current `person()` helper in `data/build_data.py` has a single `spouse` parameter; this may be insufficient for multiple marriages or concurrent partnerships. Do not overwrite one union with another or rely on one-sided spouse fields. Extend the model deliberately if needed, with relationship type, dates, source and confidence, while keeping biological parentage separate from union records. Ensure GEDCOM/export and visualisations preserve these distinctions.

**Research principle:** completeness means investigating all credible lines and connections, not lowering the evidentiary standard. A well-documented illegitimate line belongs in the history just as much as a legitimate one.

# Archived file: leads/archive-review-leads.md

# Archive review: new leads from the uploaded family-tree export

Reviewed 2026-11-16 from the text export, JSON, GEDCOM and embedded JPGs in the supplied ZIP. These notes distinguish what the export says from what is visible in the scans. The original ZIP remains preserved in this directory.

## 1. Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen — church-register entry and competing fatherhood claims

Visible in the supplied Broager Vestre district church-book scan (image 08 in the ZIP; the export identifies it as entry no. 1 in the 1932–33 register):

- Child: Lorenz Heinrich.
- Birth: 12 January 1932, Egernsund.
- The handwritten baptism date appears to be 17 January; recheck the full-resolution register if the exact date matters.
- Parents are written as worker Peter Frederiksen and wife Else Gedde Jensen, Egernsund.
- A note refers to a letter from the civil registrar (Personregisterføreren) dated 11 July 1932.

This is primary-source evidence for the parentage recorded by the church register, but it does not establish biological paternity. The separate family account that Sophus Petersen was the biological father remains a claim requiring the paternity file and/or DNA evidence. Keep legal/registered, biological and social/stepfather relationships distinct.

Other relevant scans in the ZIP:
- Sophus Petersen's 1903 Nybøl baptism entry names him as son of farmer Peter Petersen and wife Anna Cathrine Marie Petersen, née Petersen; the entry gives birth 28 June and baptism 24 July.
- The family gravestone photo gives Sofus Petersen of Paakjær's dates as 28 June 1903–24 August 1945, and names wife Christine Petersen née Jensen (1907–1985).
- The 1921 Nybøl census scan is captioned as Peter Petersen's household, with Detlef and Botilde Krogh at the top. This is regional context only; it does not prove any relationship between Wilhelm Krogh and Lorenz.

Next documentary step: obtain the 1932 paternity/maintenance case and the civil-register basis referred to by the church entry. Continue with Aabenraa-Sønderborg county archive alimentation/journal records and relevant civil court/police records already identified in the research notes. Do not change the biological father field on the strength of this church-book entry.

## 2. Jochum (Joachim) Gaardsted — Skødstrup baptism, 1792

The handwritten entry in the ZIP names “Hr. Forpagter Gaardsted paa Vosnæsgaard” and his wife Kirstine Marie Jørgensdatter; the child is named Jochum and the text appears to give 23 September as the birth date. This supports the Vosnæsgaard/Gaardsted household context. Check the full register page and catalogue metadata before using the exact date or wording to resolve identity or chronology.

The included 1839 drawing of Vosnæsgaard is contextual illustration, not proof of the 1792 household.

## 3. Liss (Lis) Pedersen — Silkeborg birth, 1931

The archive caption identifies Silkeborg church book, girls born 1931 no. 25: Lis Pedersen, born 18 April at Drewsensvej 14; parents machine worker Jens Nielsen Pedersen and Hulda Kristensen. The export links the image to Rigsarkivet image endpoint https://api.rigsarkivet.dk/ao/v1/images/28329752. Treat the caption as the supplied transcription until the original register is independently reread.

## 4. Evald Johannes Gaardsted-Jørgensen — Kolind birth, 1922

The archive caption identifies Kolind church book, boys born 1922 no. 6, with parents Aage Evald Oskar Jørgensen and Jensine Mariane Magdalene Gaardsted, and a marginal note concerning the 1985 name change. The export links the source to Arkivalieronline episode 17124596. Inspect the complete register page before transcribing the marginal note.

## 5. Gedde family photograph, 1895

The family-held photograph is captioned as Edvard Hammer Gedde and Mathilde Ludvigsen with their 12 children, apparently made as a silver-wedding gift in 1895. The caption identifies child no. 8 as Paula Mathilde Christle/Chrestle Gedde (“Laue”), later Jensen. Treat identities and birth-order mapping as family-caption identifications until independently corroborated.

## 6. Langø memoir pages

Two scans from Lisbeth Skov's En Langø-piges erindringer are captioned as describing her grandparents Marius and Ane Marie Christensen buying Langø in 1894 for 4,000 kroner and naming their children. This is a valuable family-history lead, not a substitute for the deed/tingbog or municipal records. Retain the plan to verify purchase and causeway history against land and local-government records.

## 7. Data-format checks before import

- Text export header: 489 people (98 private/living, 391 public).
- JSON people array: 489 records; 98 are redacted living/private records.
- GEDCOM: 485 individual records. The discrepancy is unexplained; do not assume identical person coverage.
- Structured JSON also reports 54 places, 7 family lines, 9 categories, 11 photos, 17 related links and 6 source groups.
- The previously identified duplicate person ID (p13) and rejected Bornholm Lars Larsen link remain data-integrity issues; do not overwrite canonical relationships without a reconciliation pass.
- The JSON also contains an encrypted private_blob. It was not decrypted or inspected and is excluded from the extracted working copy.

## Source references carried by the export

- Liss: https://api.rigsarkivet.dk/ao/v1/images/28329752
- Lorenz: https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/billedviser?epid=17216172
- Sophus: https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/billedviser?epid=17217126
- 1921 census: https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/billedviser?bsid=85824#85824,13856442
- Jochum: https://arkivalieronline.rigsarkivet.dk/da/billedviser?epid=24257049
- Vosnæsgaard drawing: https://arkiv.dk/vis/2780543

The complete export contains all 17 related-media links and the source registry.

# Archived file: leads/independent-research-2026-11.md

# Independent research and GEDCOM audit

Research date: 16 November 2026. This note records an independent comparison of the uploaded structured JSON and GEDCOM, plus external source checks. It is not a replacement tree; no unsupported parentage has been added.

## 1. GEDCOM completeness audit

The supplied JSON contains 489 person objects. The GEDCOM contains 485 individual (INDI) records. Matching the JSON person IDs against GEDCOM xrefs identifies exactly four JSON people with no GEDCOM individual record:

| JSON ID | Person | JSON source/evidence | Assessment |
|---|---|---|---|
| p74 | Karen Christensdatter | Dansk Slægtsforskning, Rolsøgård estate (G302), probate no. 11, 14 May 1787, fol. 39 | The JSON record has no dates, parents, spouse or children; no GEDCOM individual exists. Do not infer a relationship from the probate citation alone. |
| woyten_benedictus | Benedictus (Benedict) von Woyten | von Kleist family archive, English translation, p. 123 | Named as Christina von Woyten's father in the family-history source. |
| stojentin_elisabeth | Elisabeth von Stojentin | von Kleist family archive, English translation, p. 123 | Named as Christina's mother in the family-history source. |
| below_otto_pustamin | Otto von Below of Pustamin | von Kleist family archive, English translation, p. 123 | Named as Christina's earlier husband; this is a spouse relationship, not a parent relationship. |

The GEDCOM has no IDs absent from the JSON (485/485 matched). This proves the GEDCOM is a reduced export of the JSON, not a complete representation of it. It does not prove the JSON itself is complete or correct.

The three von Woyten/Stojentin/Below people are especially important: the von Kleist family archive translation states that Christina, widow of Otto von Below of Pustamin, was the daughter of Benedictus von Woyten of Versin and Elisabeth von Stojentin of Giesebitz; she later married Georg von Kleist. The export's JSON preserves these people and links, while the GEDCOM omits all three individuals. See https://v-kleist.com/FG/english/Dubberow_translation.pdf, p. 123.

Karen Christensdatter is different: she is a source-only, currently unlinked person in the JSON. The cited Rolsøgård probate index is a promising route to identifying her, but without reading the original probate and matching the people, she should remain unlinked. Source: https://slaegt.dk/media/jnnnvuml/alfabetisk-navneliste-og-skifteuddrag-til-rols%C3%B8g%C3%A5rd-gods-g302-mols-herred-1778-1816-1818.pdf.

## 2. Independent check: the Gaardsted / Vosnæsgaard line

The uploaded data identifies Jochum (Joachim) Gaardsted, born 23 September 1792 at Vosnæsgaard, as a child of Claus Gaardsted and Kirsten Marie Jørgensdatter. The archive scan caption identifies a Skødstrup baptism entry in 1792 and names “Hr. Forpagter Gaardsted paa Vosnæsgaard” and his wife Kirstine Marie Jørgensdatter. This is the key contemporary source to inspect at full resolution before treating the relationship and exact date as fully transcribed.

Independent contextual corroboration:
- Djurslandsdatabasen's surname index lists Joachim Gaardsted, born 1792 at Vosnøsgård and died 1865 in Tirstrup parish. It also lists Hans Peter Gaardsted, born 1852 in Tirstrup, and several other Gaardsted family members. This is a secondary compiled database, useful as a discovery aid, not a substitute for parish registers: https://djursslaegt.djursdatabasen.dk/efternavne/EFTERNAVNG.htm.
- Trap Danmark's Vistoft parish history says Bogensholm was bought by Claus Gaardsted in 1796 for 10,000 rigsdaler and later sold by his widow in 1811. This independently confirms the landowner Claus Gaardsted and the family/estate context, but does not itself prove he was Jochum's father: https://trap5.lex.dk/sogn/vistoft-sogn-mols-herred/.
- A contemporary royal rescript dated 6 August 1803 addresses Claus Gaardsted as owner of Bogensholm and orders him to pay the customary church offerings. This is strong contemporary evidence that a Claus Gaardsted owned Bogensholm by 1803: https://da.wikisource.org/wiki/Side:Kongelige_Rescripter,_Resolutioner_og_Collegialbreve_for_Danmark_og_Norge_udtogsvis_udgivne_i_chronologisk_Orden._VI._Deel._12._Bind_(1803%E2%80%931804).pdf/258.

A discrepancy exists in later estate histories: Trap's Vistoft parish page gives the purchase as 1796, while another indexed Trap edition/search result gives 1797. This one-year discrepancy should be resolved from the deed/auction record, not silently normalized.

## 3. Independent check: Ove Samuel Gedde

The export currently gives Ove Samuel Gedde's birth as 28 February 1778. The genealogical entry in Personalhistorisk Tidsskrift (Harbou, 1897) also gives 28 February 1778 and death 10 March 1843. This is independent published support for the date currently in the tree: https://v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf.

A conflicting 1885 biography by Carl Johan Anker gives 28 May 1788. The digitized page is explicitly marked as not proofread OCR and the entry appears to confuse Ove Samuel with his father Hans Christopher Gedde. Do not replace the 1778 date on the strength of that OCR. Original baptism evidence remains the preferred confirmation. Source: https://arkivkopia.se/bok/runeberg-acj330bio.

## 4. Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen — what the independent research can and cannot establish

Rigsarkivet's current guidance confirms that cases from 1919–1970 began with the police; disputed paternity was decided by the civil court; maintenance/alimentation files are held in the county archive. It recommends starting with the county's alimentation journal, ordinary journal and case files, then checking civil-court case lists/judgment books and police records if needed: https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/vejledning/faderskab-og-adoption/faderskabssager-kom-godt-i-gang/.

The archive overview for Landsarkivet for Sønderjylland lists Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt archives from 1932 onward, and says the 1920–1935 journal registers, journals and case files require an unprinted reading-room finding aid. This makes the Aabenraa-Sønderborg county archive a concrete next search location for the July 1932 registrar's letter and any associated paternity/maintenance case: https://www.rigsarkivet.dk/wp-content/uploads/2022/06/Landsarkivet-for-Soenderjylland-En-arkivoversigt.pdf.

I did not locate the actual paternity case, the 10 June 1932 judgment, or the complete underlying civil-register file in this web pass. Accordingly, the biological-father hypothesis (Sophus Petersen) remains unresolved. The church-register entry names Peter Frederiksen and Else Gedde Jensen as parents and refers to the registrar's letter; it is evidence of the register's recorded parentage, not proof of biological paternity.

## 5. Next research sequence

1. Obtain and inspect the original Skødstrup 1792 baptism page and trace Jochum through marriage, census and death records; then independently verify the parentage of Hans Peter Gaardsted (1852) and Jensine Gaardsted (1894).
2. Request the Aabenraa-Sønderborg Amt journal-register/journal and relevant case file for the July 1932 registrar's letter and Lorenz's paternity/maintenance proceedings. Search under Else Gedde Jensen, Peter Frederiksen, Wilhelm Krogh and Lorenz Heinrich Frederiksen, allowing for spelling variants.
3. Inspect the Rolsøgård 1787 probate folio 39 to identify Karen Christensdatter and determine whether she belongs in any existing line.
4. Preserve the von Woyten/Stojentin/Below relationships as source-supported in the JSON; if regenerating GEDCOM, include the three missing individuals and encode Christina's earlier marriage separately from her later marriage to Georg von Kleist.
5. Before any GEDCOM regeneration, audit every JSON person, parent, spouse, step/foster relationship, source and note against the export rules. A count match alone is insufficient.

## Method and limitations

- Compared 489 JSON person IDs with 485 GEDCOM INDI xrefs: four missing JSON people, zero GEDCOM-only people.
- External sources were used as corroboration and research directions, not as automatic imports.
- No new canonical relationship was written to the application data.
- The encrypted/private payload was not decrypted or inspected.

### Gerda Frederiksen (f. Larsen)
ID: p7
Sex: F
Born: 17 August 1935, Magleby (Nordenbro), Langeland, Denmark
Died: 18 July 2019, Svendborg, Denmark
Occupations: none recorded

Notes: Born 17 Aug 1935 in Nordenbro, Magleby parish on South Langeland, and
       home-baptised the next day. Presented in Magleby Kirke 3 Nov 1935.
       Parents: carpenter Laurits Edvard Larsen and Karen Margrethe Holgersen.
       Godparents: farmer Johannes Nielsen and wife, Haugbølle, and cooper
       Aksel Holgersen, Kædeby (her maternal grandfather). Through her maternal
       grandmother Kristine she is a great-granddaughter of Marius Christensen,
       'Kongen af Langø' ('the King of Langø'), who bought the island of Langø
       in Lindelse Nor in 1894 and in 1911 built the causeway to Langeland.
       Buried at Sankt Jørgens Kirkegård in Svendborg with her husband Lorenz.
       Confirmed 16 April 1950; married 25 Nov 1954 in Magleby Kirke (family
       chart). The MyHeritage tree gives death year 2019.

### Marius Michelsen Christensen ("Kongen af Langø")
ID: p62
Sex: M
Born: 14 April 1858, Rudkøbing, Denmark
Died: [not recorded]
Occupations over time:
- Landbruger og ejer af øen Langø (1894–) (farmer and owner of Langø)
- Bygger af Langø-dæmningen (1911) (builder of the Langø causeway)
- Arbejdsmand ved Rudkøbing havn og Frihavnen i København (labourer at
    Rudkøbing harbour and Copenhagen Free Port)
- Arbejder på Langelands Korn (worker at Langelands Korn)
- Ejer af Langø fra 1894 (owner of Langø from 1894)
Locations over time:
  1858 Rudkøbing — born
  1883 Rudkøbing — married
  1894 Langø, Lindelse Nor — bought Langø
  1921 Langø, Lindelse Nor — 'Kongen af Langø'
Notes: 'Kongen af Langø'. He bought the island of Langø in Lindelse Nor in 1894
       with his wife Ane Marie, and in 1911 he built, by hand with help from
       sons and sons-in-law, the 334 m long causeway that made the island
       landfast. According to the family, Langø later passed to descendants
       named Pedersen. Several of the family's relatives from South Langeland
       emigrated to America. Granddaughter Lisbeth Skov, née Christensen,
       relates in 'En Langø-piges erindringer' that before 1894 the island had
       belonged to Hjortholm estate and later to three farms in Haugbølle,
       which used it for grazing: cattle were swum over in spring and home in
       autumn. A shoemaker had bought the island and built a simple house, but
       it went to forced auction, after which Marius and Ane Marie bought it.
       From granddaughter Lisbeth Skov's memoirs: Marius was in farming and a
       labourer, helped build Rudkøbing harbour and then helped dig the
       Copenhagen Free Port (he found a cannonball in the harbour, still on
       Langø). Back in Rudkøbing the couple bought a small smallholding on
       Spodsbjergvej, and Marius worked at Langelands Korn for 2 kroner a day.
       The owner of Baygården, chairman of Langelands Bank, who had taken over
       Langø at forced auction, persuaded them to buy the island for 4,000
       kroner with 1,600 kroner down. In 1894 the family moved out there with
       four children. From 11 Jan 1911 Marius and the sons built a causeway to
       Kædeby Haver, eventually 334 metres long and 6–8 metres wide; the
       authorities did not think he would finish it in his, his children's or
       grandchildren's time.
Spouse: Ane Marie — p63
Children: Kristine Adamine Margrethe Christensen — p31 (and others)
Father: [not in JSON]
Mother: [not in JSON]
Sources: LL:12-15570702; LL:13-5085956; LL:9-1827830; Lisbeth Skov f.
         Christensen: 'En Langø-piges erindringer'

## Section 9 — EARLIER GENERATIONS (9–14) — GEDDE & VON KLEIST LINE
---

The tree's deepest documented line runs through the Gedde and von Kleist
families. The source material here is the family-tree.md narrative and the
GEDCOM; individual JSON entries for these generations are sparse and largely
without dates.

Key named ancestors on the Gedde / von Kleist line include:
- Cartz Ulrik von Gedde (III.369) — Danish officer, went into Danish service
    in 1701, became lieutenant-colonel, died in Rendsborg in 1722.
- Pribislaff von Kleist (III.281) of Muttrin, Borntin, Döbel and Drenow —
    died 1698; married A. E. von Kameke. Identified by Harbou (1897) and the
    newer von Kleist register as the father of Cartz Ulrik.
- Pribislaff (d. 1640) — earlier Borntin line.
- Prebislaw til Borntin — mentioned 1621.
- Hans Jürgen — landråd (councillor), son of Prebislaw.
- Prebislaw til Muttrin, Borntin og Drenow — son of Hans Jürgen.
- Prissebur / Klest de Densin — medieval mentions: 1248 Conradus and
    Bertoldus Clest in a Pomeranian document; 1289 Klest (Klest de Densin)
    in a fief/witness document; 1364 mentioned as co-owner of land in Glineke
    and called Klest from Muttrin; 1477 Duke Bogislaw X's fief letter lists
    several Kleist branches and reproduces the family's declaration of a common
    ancestor and three sons.

Important caveat from the source:
  "The precise chain from Pribislaff von Kleist (d. 1698) through the Borntin
   branch to Prissebur must still be verified in the revised family history and
   the underlying fief letters. The medieval persons should therefore not yet be
   entered as direct ancestors in the tree."

Sources for the von Kleist line:
- H. W. Harbou, "Slægten von Kleist i Danmark" (1897):
    https://www.v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf
- "Muttrin-Damensche historie" (2025):
    https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Muttrin/Muttrin.pdf
- "Familien von Kleists dokumentbog":
    https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/Gesamt.pdf
- Overview of the early documents:
    https://www.v-kleist.com/FG/urkunden/urkunden.htm

## Section 12 — NOTES FOR THE AGENT
---

1. Living persons are intentionally redacted. Names of anyone living, or born
   within the last 100 years without a death date, are AES-256-GCM encrypted in
   data/private.enc and appear as "Privat person" on the public site. The
   password is not in the public repo. If the agent needs living-person data,
   it must be supplied separately by the user.

2. Two different people share the ID "p13" in the JSON: Else Gedde Jensen
   (maternal line) and Lovisa Larsson (paternal Jørgensen line). This is a
   quirk of the export; the context distinguishes them.

3. The "sofus_f" and "sofus_m" entries are the parents of Sophus Petersen.
   The "sofus" entry is Sophus Petersen himself.

4. The Bornholm branch (Lars Larsen b. 1771 Rutsker, d. 1854 Allinge) is
   explicitly rejected in the source as an erroneous automatic link and is
   therefore not part of this export.

5. The medieval von Kleist ancestors are documented in the narrative but are
   not yet entered as direct ancestors in the structured tree because the
   chain of proof is incomplete.

6. For images: the raw.githubusercontent.com URLs above are stable and
   fetchable. The GitHub blob URLs (github.com/sloev/tree/blob/main/images/...)
   also work but return HTML; the raw URLs are better for agent ingestion.


END OF EXPORT

A few things worth flagging:

· Living people are redacted. Names of anyone living, or born within the last 100 years without a death date, are encrypted in data/private.enc. The site shows them as "Privat person". If your agent needs those names, you'll need to provide the decryption password separately — it's not in the public repo.
· Two people share the ID p13 in the JSON (Else Gedde Jensen on the maternal line, Lovisa Larsson on the paternal Jørgensen line). This is an export quirk; context distinguishes them.
· The Bornholm branch is deliberately excluded. The site's own notes reject the link to Lars Larsen b. 1771 Rutsker as an erroneous automatic connection.
· Medieval von Kleist ancestors are documented but not yet treated as proven direct ancestors. The source says the chain from Pribislaff von Kleist (d. 1698) back to the 13th–15th century Kleist mentions "must still be verified" — so they're listed in Section 9 as context, not as confirmed tree members.
· The images/ folder has 9 files, all mapped to the people they relate to above. The raw URLs are directly fetchable by your agent.

# Research progress — 16 November 2026

This is a dated working note, not a canonical-tree update. It records checks made against the live `leads/` folder and the first external-source pass. Unresolved claims remain leads.

## Repository re-check

The live `leads/` directory was re-listed before research. It currently contains the source register, branch dossiers, worklist, research notes, potential-leads register, three extension reviews, the original ZIP, and the supplied extension segments through Segment 15. The latest `leads/README.md`, `investigation-worklist.md`, `file-up7.file` (Segment 13), `file-m4d.file` (Segment 14), `file-dk8.file` (Segment 15), and `review-final-extension-batch-2026-11.md` were reopened. The supplied segment files were not edited.

## Harbou audit — first verified rows

### Source opened

H. W. Harbou, “Slægten von Kleist i Danmark,” *Personalhistorisk Tidsskrift* (1897), beginning at p. 95. The accessible transcription is:

- https://v-kleist.com/FG_allg/Kleist_in_Daenemark.pdf
- The text identifies itself as Harbou's 1897 article and explains that he supplemented the German family history with Danish-source material. Its introductory note says the online text was transcribed from a Google Books edition with ABBYY Danish OCR and genealogical numbers were added from the family history. It is therefore a useful secondary source and numbering aid, but OCR must be checked against page images for ambiguous names and dates.

### Rows directly located in Harbou
